\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{P}_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\right)[T_{p}(\log\tilde{q}_{t-1},X_{t-1},m_{t}(X_{t}))]=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\right).

Proof.

See
Section

H.4
.
∎

Thus, with the help of
Lemmas

3
and
4
, we can identify the dominating terms in
\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish, rev-step}}
when
1-\alpha_{t}
is small. The dominating term is

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish, rev-step}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{P}_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\right)[\tilde{\zeta}_{\text{van}}(X_{t},X_{t-1})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[(\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3!}\left(\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\right)\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\bigg[3\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{j}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+O((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}).

(18)

Therefore, with the decomposition in (
15
) in mind, an upper bound on the reverse-step error is achieved by summing up (
16
), (
17
) and (
18
). The proof of
Theorem

1
is now complete.

F.5
Corollary

1
and its proof

Below we state and prove a corollary of
Theorem

1
when
\tilde{q}_{0}
does not exist. By
[
25
, Lemma 6]
,
\tilde{q}_{1}
always exists, which provides us with the following convergence result with early-stopping.

Corollary 1
.

Suppose that
Assumptions

1
,
3
,
4
and
2
are satisfied. Then, suppose that the
\alpha_{t}
satisfies
Definition

1
at
t\geq 2
, the distribution
\widehat{P}_{1}
from the discrete-time DDPM under score bias satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{Q}_{1}\|\widehat{P}_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=2}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\bigg[(\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\left(\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})-\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{\intercal}\right)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[3\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{j}\right]

\displaystyle+\max_{t\geq 2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}(\log T)\varepsilon+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2},

where
\mathrm{W}_{2}(\tilde{Q}_{1},\tilde{Q}_{0})^{2}\lesssim(1-\alpha_{1})d
.

Proof.

The result directly follows with the same arguments as in the proof of
Theorem

1
. The only difference is the guarantee under the Wasserstein distance, which can be obtained using
[
25
, Lemma 12]
.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of
Theorem

2

We first recall some of the properties of the noise schedule in (
8
). By
Lemma

6
, the noise schedule in (
8
) satisfies that
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p}}\lesssim\frac{\log T\log(1/\delta)}{\delta^{p-1}T}
for all
p\geq 1
while
\bar{\alpha}_{T}=o(T^{-1})
, and thus such
\alpha_{t}
satisfies
Definition

1
when
t\geq 2
.
Further, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
),
[
25
, Lemmas 15 and 17]
show that for
any
Q_{0}
with finite variance under early-stopping,
\forall p,\ell\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{p}\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right|^{\ell}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{p\ell/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{p}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\right|^{\ell}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p\ell/2}}\right).

Thus, using
Lemma

6
,
Assumption

3
is satisfied (since
\delta
is constant). In the following, we further verify the last relationship in
Assumption

3
holds.

Therefore, since
1
,
2
and
4
have been satisfied, we can invoke
Corollary

1
and get
\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{Q}_{1}\|\widehat{P}_{1})\lesssim\mathcal{W}_{\text{oracle}}+\mathcal{W}_{\text{bias}}+\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
.
Now, we investigate the dimensional dependence for each term of the upper bound in
Corollary

1
.

To start, from
[
25
, Theorem 3]
, for any
\tilde{Q}_{0}
having finite variance, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
), we have

\mathcal{W}_{\text{oracle}}\lesssim\frac{d^{2}\log^{2}(1/\delta)(\log T)^{2}}{T}+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2}.

Also, since by assumption
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{r}}d^{\gamma}
, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
), we have from
Lemma

7
that when
\delta\ll 1
,

\mathcal{W}_{\text{bias}}\lesssim\frac{d^{\gamma}}{\delta^{r}}\left(1-\frac{2\log(1/\delta)\log T}{T}\right).

Now we investigate each term in
\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
. The following lemma is useful to determine the rate of difference of the first-order Taylor polynomials.

Lemma 5
.

When
\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim d^{3}
, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
), we have

(1-\alpha_{t})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\lesssim\frac{d^{3/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}.

As a result,
Assumption

3
holds.

Proof.

See
Section

H.5
.
∎

In other words, combining
Lemma

5
and
Lemma

6
, we have

(1-\alpha_{t})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

(19)

Now, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and
Lemma

5
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\bigg[(\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{\gamma/2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{r/2}}\times\frac{d^{3/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{d^{\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{\delta^{1+r/2}T^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d^{\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{\delta^{1+r/2}T}.

To proceed for higher orders of Taylor polynomials, we first note that from
[
25
, Section G.2]
, the second and third derivatives of
\log\tilde{q}_{t}
are

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}I_{d}+\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(\cdot|x)}\left[(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})^{\intercal}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(\cdot|x)}\left[x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\right]\right)\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(\cdot|x)}\left[x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\right]\right)^{\intercal}\bigg)

\displaystyle\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=-\int z^{i}z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1}=i,j,k\\
a_{2}<a_{3},\penalty\ a_{2},a_{3}\neq a_{1}\end{subarray}}\int z^{a_{1}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{a_{2}}z^{a_{3}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int z^{i}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{j}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{k}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)

where
z:=\frac{x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}
.
Thus, in order to provide an upper bound on the expected norm of the second-order derivative of
\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})
, we can calculate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t})}\left[(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0})(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0})^{\intercal}\right]\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t},X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t})}\left\lVert(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0})(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0})^{\intercal}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t},X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t})}\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}\right\rVert^{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\lesssim}}d^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}}\left[m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}\right]\right)\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}}\left[m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}\right]\right)^{\intercal}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t})}\left[m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}\right]\right\rVert^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t},X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t})}\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}\right\rVert^{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\lesssim}}d^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2},

where both
(i)
and
(ii)
follow from
[
25
, Lemma 16]
.
Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}d^{2}.

For third-order derivatives, we can similarly use
[
25
, Lemma 16]
and get (cf.
[
25
, Section G.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t})))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}(Z^{i})^{6}\lesssim\frac{d}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t})))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\left(\mathbb{E}(Z^{i})^{6}\right)^{2/3}\left(\mathbb{E}(Z^{j})^{6}\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}.

Here we denote
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
, and note that
\mathbb{E}(Z^{i})^{6},\mathbb{E}(Z^{j})^{6}\lesssim 1
.

Therefore, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\frac{d^{\gamma}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{r}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\frac{d^{\gamma}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{r}}\sqrt{\frac{d^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{1+\gamma}\log(1/\delta)^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{\delta^{r-1}T},

and

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[3\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{i}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{3(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t})))^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=2}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\frac{d^{\gamma/2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{r/2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{\delta^{\frac{r-1}{2}}T},

and, with
M
being a matrix such that
M^{ij}(x):=\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(x))
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{j}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert M(X_{t})\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert M(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t})))^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=2}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\frac{d^{(1+\gamma)/2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{r/2}}\sqrt{\frac{d^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{\delta^{\frac{r-1}{2}}T},

and

\max_{t\geq 2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}(\log T)\varepsilon\lesssim\frac{d^{\gamma/2}}{\delta^{r/2}}(\log T)\varepsilon.

Therefore, combining all the above, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{Q}_{1}\|\widehat{P}_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{\gamma}\delta^{-r}\left(1-\frac{2\log(1/\delta)\log T}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\max\{d^{(3+\gamma)/2}\delta^{-\frac{r+2}{2}},d^{1+\gamma}\delta^{-(r-1)}\}\frac{(\log T)^{2}}{T}

\displaystyle\qquad+d^{\gamma/2}\delta^{-r/2}(\log T)\varepsilon.

Appendix H
Auxiliary Lemmas and Proofs in
Section

3

H.1
Proof of
Lemma

1

We remind readers that throughout this proof
x_{t}
is fixed. For brevity write
m_{t}=m_{t}(x_{t})
,
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}(x_{t})
, and
\Delta_{t}(x_{t})=\Delta_{t}
. Recall that
m_{t}=x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}+(1-\alpha_{t})/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})
.
By Bayes’ rule, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{q}_{t|t-1}(x_{t}|x_{t-1})\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})}{\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})}

\displaystyle\propto\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})\tilde{q}_{t|t-1}(x_{t}|x_{t-1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\propto}}\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t-1}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle\propto\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})p_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\frac{\left\lVert x_{t-1}-\mu_{t}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})p_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\frac{\left\lVert x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}+x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}-\mu_{t}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\propto\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})p_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\frac{(x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})^{\intercal}(x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}-\mu_{t})}{(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}\right)

where
(i)
follows because the forward process is Markov and
\tilde{q}_{t|t-1}=q_{t|t-1}
.
Here, the exponent is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{(x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})^{\intercal}(x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}-\mu_{t})}{(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})^{\intercal}(m_{t}-\mu_{t})}{(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}-\frac{(x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})^{\intercal}((1-\alpha_{t})/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})}{(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\Delta_{t}^{\intercal}(x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}(x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})^{\intercal}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t}).

Thus,

\tilde{q}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\propto p_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\tilde{\zeta}_{t,t-1}(x_{t},x_{t-1})\right)

where

\tilde{\zeta}_{t,t-1}(x_{t},x_{t-1})=\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\Delta_{t}^{\intercal}(x_{t-1}-m_{t})+\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}(x_{t-1}-m_{t})^{\intercal}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t}).

Finally, since all partial derivatives of
\tilde{q}_{t-1}
exists for any
t\geq 2
(See
[
25
, Lemma 6]
), the Taylor expansion of
\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}
around
m_{t}
gives the desirable result.

H.2
Proof of
Lemma

2

For each
t=1,\dots,T
,

\displaystyle\log\frac{p_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})}{\widehat{p}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\left(\left\lVert x_{t-1}-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(x_{t}))^{\intercal}(\mu_{t}(x_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(x_{t}))+\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(x_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}.

For the first term above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t,t-1}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[(m_{t}(X_{t})-\mu_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left[\Delta_{t}(X_{t})^{\intercal}(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}.

Here we recall the definition of
\Delta_{t}
from (
2
) where
m_{t}(x)-\mu_{t}(x)=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}(x)
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t,t-1}}\left[\log\frac{p_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}{\widehat{p}_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t\geq 1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\frac{\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\lesssim}}\max_{t\geq 1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}(\log T)\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\lesssim}}\max_{t\geq 1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}(\log T)\varepsilon+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2}

where
(i)
follows from

Definition

1
,
(ii)
follows from the fact that for any non-negative sequence
a_{t}
,
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sqrt{a_{t}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}a_{t}}
by Jensen’s inequality, and
(iii)
follows from
Assumption

2
. The proof is complete.

H.3
Proof of
Lemma

3

Recall that
P_{t-1|t}=\mathcal{N}(\mu_{t},\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{d})
, and thus
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}[X_{t-1}-m_{t}(x_{t})]=-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}(x_{t})
.
Note that we can change the limit and the expectation under
Assumption

3
.
Now, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}[\tilde{\zeta}_{\text{van}}(x_{t},X_{t-1})]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}(\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(x_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t}))^{\intercal}\Delta_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=2}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[T_{p}(\log\tilde{q}_{t-1},X_{t-1},m_{t}(x_{t}))\right].

Below we write
m_{t}=m_{t}(x_{t})
. Since
T_{2}
is in quadratic form, the expected value under
P_{t-1|t}
for this term is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[T_{2}(\log\tilde{q}_{t-1},X_{t-1},m_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathrm{Tr}(\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}))+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\Delta_{t}(x_{t})^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\Delta_{t}(x_{t}).

Recall the formula for Gaussian non-centralized third moment. If
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu,\sigma^{2})
, then
\mathbb{E}[Z^{2}]=\mu^{2}+\sigma^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}[Z^{3}]=\mu^{3}+3\mu\sigma^{2}
. Thus, the expected value under
P_{t-1|t}
for
T_{3}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[T_{3}(\log\tilde{q}_{t-1},X_{t-1},m_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{2}(X_{t-1}^{j}-m_{t}^{j})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k=1\\
i,j,k\text{ all differ}\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})(X_{t-1}^{j}-m_{t}^{j})(X_{t-1}^{k}-m_{t}^{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\left(\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{i}\right)^{3}+3\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{i}\right)\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\left(\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{i}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)\right)\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k=1\\
i,j,k\text{ all differ}\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{i}\right)\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{j}\right)\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3!}\left(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\right)\left(3\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\Delta_{t}^{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\Delta_{t}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\right),\penalty\ \forall\ell\geq 1.

Here the last line follows because
(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\left|\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\right|=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\right)
under
Assumption

3
and
(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})
under
Assumption

4
, both for all
\ell\geq 1
. The proof is now complete.

H.4
Proof of
Lemma

4

Fix
x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For brevity write
m_{t}=m_{t}(x_{t})
,
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}(x_{t})
, and
\Delta_{t}=\Delta_{t}(x_{t})
. Recall that

T_{p}(\log\tilde{q}_{t-1},x_{t-1},m_{t})=\frac{1}{p!}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}:\sum_{i}\gamma^{i}=p}\partial^{p}_{\bm{a}}\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\prod_{i=1}^{d}(x_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}

where
\bm{a}\in[d]^{p}
are the indices of differentiation in which the multiplicity of
i
is
\gamma^{i}
.
First, for the expectation under
\tilde{P}_{t-1|t}
(i.e., Gaussian centralized moments),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{P}_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{d}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{P}_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{\gamma^{i}/2}(\gamma^{i}-1)!!\mathds{1}\{\gamma^{i}\text{ is even}\}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{p/2}\prod_{i=1}^{d}(\gamma^{i}-1)!!\mathds{1}\{\gamma^{i}\text{ is even}\},

where we use the convention that
(-1)!!=1
.
Next, for the expectation under
P_{t-1|t}
(i.e., Gaussian non-centralized moments),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{d}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=0\\
\ell\text{ even}\end{subarray}}^{\gamma^{i}}\binom{\gamma^{i}}{\ell}\left(-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Delta_{t}^{i}\right)^{\gamma^{i}-\ell}\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{\ell/2}(\ell-1)!!

To investigate their difference, we divide into the following few cases. Note that under
Assumption

4
,
(1-\alpha_{t})^{m}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{m})
for any
m\geq 1/2
and
\ell\geq 1
.

1.

Case 1:
p
is even and all elements of
\gamma^{i}
are even. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{d}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{d}\left(\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{\gamma^{i}/2}(\gamma^{i}-1)!!+O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{\gamma^{i}/2+1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{p/2}\prod_{i=1}^{d}(\gamma^{i}-1)!!+O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{p/2+1}\right)

2.

Case 2:
p
is even and
\exists i^{*}
such that
\gamma^{i^{*}}
is odd. Since
\sum_{i}\gamma^{i}=p
, there exists
j^{*}
such that
\gamma^{j^{*}}
is also odd. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i^{*}}-m_{t}^{i^{*}})^{\gamma^{i^{*}}}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left(\left(1-\alpha_{t}\right)^{(\gamma^{i^{*}}+1)/2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{j^{*}}-m_{t}^{j^{*}})^{\gamma^{j^{*}}}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left(\left(1-\alpha_{t}\right)^{(\gamma^{j^{*}}+1)/2}\right),

which implies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{d}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left(\left(1-\alpha_{t}\right)^{p/2+1}\right)

3.

Case 3:
p
is odd and
\exists i^{*}
such that
\gamma^{i^{*}}
is odd. Then,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i^{*}}-m_{t}^{i^{*}})^{\gamma^{i^{*}}}\right]=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left(\left(1-\alpha_{t}\right)^{(\gamma^{i^{*}}+1)/2}\right),

which implies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{d}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]=O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left(\left(1-\alpha_{t}\right)^{(p+1)/2}\right)

Combining these cases, we get

\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim\tilde{P}_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[\prod_{i=1}^{d}(X_{t-1}^{i}-m_{t}^{i})^{\gamma^{i}}\right]\\
=\begin{cases}O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{\frac{p}{2}+1}\right),&\forall p\geq 4\penalty\ \text{even}\\
O_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}(\tilde{Q}_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right),&\forall p\geq 4\penalty\ \text{odd}\end{cases}

The proof is complete by noting that the rate does not change when we take the expectation over
\tilde{Q}_{t}
under
3
and
4
.

H.5
Proof of
Lemma

5

Note that
\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x|x_{0})=q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})
is the p.d.f. of
\mathcal{N}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0},(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d})
.
Thus, the gradient of
\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x)
equals

\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x)=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x_{0})}{\tilde{q}_{t}(x)}=-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x).

(20)

Thus,

\displaystyle\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\Bigg(\left(\frac{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}-1\right)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\Big(\left(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}-(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})\right)\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\Bigg(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})}_{\text{term 1}}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(m_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t})}_{\text{term 2}}+\underbrace{\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})}_{\text{term 3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})-\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})\right)}_{\text{term 4}}

(21)

where
(i)
follows from Tweedie’s formula. Among the four terms in (
21
), the first term satisfies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}

by
[
25
, Lemma 17]
. In the second term in (
21
), by Tweedie’s formula,

\displaystyle m_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}(x_{t}+\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})).

Thus, by
[
25
, Lemma 15]
and
Assumption

1
, the second term satisfies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(m_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}.

The third term in (
21
) satisfies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}

by Jensen’s inequality and
Assumption

1
.

To deal with the last term in (
21
), note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|x_{0})}{\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x_{0})=\frac{\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|x_{0})}{\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(y)}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x_{0})}{\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x_{0})=\frac{\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x_{0})}{\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|y)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(y)}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x_{0}).

Thus, the last term in (
21
) is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})-\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\cdot\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})}\left(\int_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x(\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|y)-\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y))\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(y)\right)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|y)-\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)

\displaystyle=\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)\left(\frac{\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|y)}{\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)\times

\displaystyle\quad\left(\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}\right)-1\right)

\displaystyle=:\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)(e^{\Delta}-1)

in which we have defined the exponent as
\Delta
. Now,

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}(x-y)^{\intercal}m_{t}+\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}\left\lVert y\right\rVert^{2}-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}-\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(x-y)^{\intercal}x_{t}+\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert y\right\rVert^{2}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(1-\alpha_{t})}{\alpha_{t}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}x_{t}+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}(1-\alpha_{t})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})\right)^{\intercal}(x-y)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}(\left\lVert y\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}).

Now, with the
\alpha_{t}
defined in (
8
), following from
Lemma

6
,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(1-\alpha_{t})}{\alpha_{t}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right).

Thus, for fixed
x,y,x_{t}
,
\Delta\to 0
as
T\to\infty
, and thus when
T
becomes large,

e^{\Delta}-1=\Delta+O(\Delta^{2})\lesssim\left|\Delta\right|,\quad\forall x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Also, since
\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)
and
\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)
decay exponentially in terms of
x
and
y
(for any fixed
x_{t}
), we have

\displaystyle\int\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\mathrm{poly}(x)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\int\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)\mathrm{poly}(y)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(y)

\displaystyle<\infty.

Thus, the limit and the integral can be exchanged due to Dominated Convergence Theorem. Thus, the fourth term in (
21
) gives us

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|m_{t})-\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{0}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\cdot\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t})\tilde{q}_{t}(x_{t})}\left(\int_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}x\tilde{q}_{t|0}(x_{t}|x)\tilde{q}_{t-1|0}(m_{t}|y)\left|\Delta\right|\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(y)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\left(\int_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x\cdot\left|\Delta\right|)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x|x_{t})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(y|m_{t})\right)

and, from definition of
\Delta
and using Cauchy-Schwartz and Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\int_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x\cdot\left|\Delta\right|)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(x|X_{t})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(y|m_{t}(X_{t}))\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}\\
X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(\cdot|X_{t})\\
Y\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t}(X_{t}))\end{subarray}}\bigg[\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{2}\bigg((\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad(\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{2})+(\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{4}+\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{4})\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}\\
X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t}(\cdot|X_{t})\\
Y\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t}(X_{t}))\end{subarray}}\bigg[\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{4}(\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{2}(\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{6}+\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{4}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\left(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{6}\right)^{2/3}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}(\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{6}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{6}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{6})\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{6}\right)^{1/3}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}\\
Y\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t}(X_{t}))\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{6}\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}(\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{6}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{6}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{6})\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{6}+\left(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{6}\right)^{1/3}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}\\
Y\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t}(X_{t}))\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{6}\right)^{2/3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{d^{3}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}},

where
(ii)
follows because, following
[
25
, Lemmas 15–17]
and by the lemma assumption that
\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim d^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim d^{3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{6}\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\tilde{Q}_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\right\rVert^{6}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{W}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left\lVert\bar{W}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim d^{3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}\\
Y\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t}(X_{t}))\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}\\
Y\sim\tilde{Q}_{0|t-1}(\cdot|m_{t}(X_{t}))\end{subarray}}\left\lVert m_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}Y\right\rVert^{6}+\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert m_{t}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim d^{3}.

Hence, combining the rates of all parts, we obtain that

(1-\alpha_{t})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim\tilde{Q}_{t}}\left\lVert\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t-1}(m_{t}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log\tilde{q}_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}\lesssim\frac{d^{3/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}.

H.6
Lemma

6
and its proof

Lemma 6
.

The
\alpha_{t}
defined in (
8
) (with
c>1
) satisfy

\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p}}\lesssim\frac{\log T\log(1/\delta)}{\delta^{p-1}T}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{while}\penalty\ \penalty\ \bar{\alpha}_{T}=o(T^{-1}),\quad\forall 2\leq t\leq T,\penalty\ p\geq 1.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
36
, Eq (39)]
. We first prove the second relationship. First, note that if
T
is large,

\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\frac{T}{\log T}}\asymp\delta e^{c}>1.

Thus, with any fixed
r\in(0,1)
such that
t\geq rT\penalty\ (\geq\frac{T}{\log T})
, we have

1-\alpha_{t}=\frac{c\log T}{T}\min\left\{\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t},1\right\}=\frac{c\log T}{T}.

As a result,

\bar{\alpha}_{T}\leq\prod_{t=\left\lfloor rT\right\rfloor}^{T}\alpha_{t}=\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\left\lceil(1-r)T\right\rceil}\asymp\exp\left(\left\lceil(1-r)T\right\rceil\left(-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\right)=O(T^{-(1-r)c}).

(22)

Given any
c>1
, we can always find some
r
such that
(1-r)c>1
(say,
r=(c-1)/2
if
c\in(1,2)
and
r=1/4
if
c\geq 2
). This shows that
\alpha_{t}
satisfies
\bar{\alpha}_{T}=o\left(T^{-1}\right)
if
c>1
.

Now, for the first relatinoship, define
\tau
such that

\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\tau}\leq 1<\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\tau+1}.

(23)

Here
\tau
is unique since
1-\alpha_{t}
is non-decreasing. In other words,
\tau
is the last time that
1-\alpha_{t}
is exponentially growing.
Assume that
T
is large enough such that
\tau\geq 2
. Below, we show that

1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t},\quad\forall 2\leq t\leq\tau.

(24)

If
t=2
,

1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}=1-\bar{\alpha}_{1}=1-\alpha_{1}=\delta\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right).

Here the last inequality holds when
T
is sufficiently large. For
t>2
, suppose for purpose of contradiction that there exists
2<t_{0}\leq\tau
such that

1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}<\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}}\penalty\ \text{while}\penalty\ 1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t},\penalty\ \forall 2\leq t\leq t_{0}-1.

In words,
t_{0}
is defined as the
first
time that (
24
) is violated. To arrive at a contradiction, we first write

\displaystyle 1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}

\displaystyle=(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2})\left(1+\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}-1}\left(1+\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}\right).

Here the inequality holds because
t_{0}
is the first time that (
24
) is violated, and thus (
24
) stills holds for
t=t_{0}-1
. Also,

1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}\leq 1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{<}}\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{2}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{2}

where
(i)
holds because (
24
) is violated at
t=t_{0}
,
(ii)
holds when
T
is sufficiently large, and
(iii)
holds because
t_{0}-1\leq\tau
and by the definition of
\tau
in (
23
). Thus,

\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{2}\frac{c\log T}{T}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}-1}}{\frac{1}{2}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}-1}}=\frac{c\log T}{T},

and thus

1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}-1}\left(1+\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t_{0}}.

We have reached a contradiction. Therefore, we have shown that (
24
) holds.

Now, (
24
) implies that

1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t/p},\quad\forall 2\leq t\leq\tau.

There are two cases:

•

If
2\leq t\leq\tau
, then

\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p}}\leq\frac{\frac{c\log T}{T}\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}}{\frac{1}{3^{p}}\delta^{p}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}}=\frac{3^{p}c\log T}{\delta^{p-1}T}.

•

If
t>\tau
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p}}\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{\tau-1})^{p}}\leq\frac{\frac{c\log T}{T}}{\frac{1}{3^{p}}\delta^{p}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\tau}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{c\log T}{T}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)}{3^{-p}\delta^{p-1}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\tau+1}}

\displaystyle<\frac{3^{p}c\log T}{\delta^{p-1}T}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right).

In both cases, if
T
is large enough, noting that
c\gtrsim\log(1/\delta)
, we have

\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p}}\leq\frac{4^{p}c\log T}{\delta^{p-1}T}\lesssim\frac{\log T\log(1/\delta)}{\delta^{p-1}T},\quad\forall 2\leq t\leq T

because
p
and
c
are constants (that do not depend on
T
,
d
, and
\delta
). The proof is now complete.
∎

H.7
Lemma

7
and its proof

Lemma 7
.

With the
\alpha_{t}
defined in (
8
), given any
p>0
, if
\delta p<1
,

\sum_{t=2}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}\leq\left(\frac{1}{p}(1-\delta)^{p}e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}+(1-\delta)^{p}\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)\right).

Further, when
\delta\ll 1
,

\sum_{t=2}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}\leq\frac{1}{p}-\left(1+\frac{p+1}{2p}\right)\frac{c\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

Proof.

Define the sum as
s_{T}
. Recall that

1-\alpha_{1}=\delta,\quad 1-\alpha_{t}=\frac{c\log T}{T}\min\left\{\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t},1\right\},\penalty\ \forall 2\leq t\leq T.

We first note a relationship that for fixed
\delta\neq 0
and
p>0
. As
z\to\infty
,

(1-\delta z^{-1})^{pz}=e^{pz\log(1-\delta z^{-1})}=e^{pz(-\delta z^{-1}+\delta^{2}z^{-2}/2+O(z^{-3}))}=e^{-\delta p}(1+\delta^{2}pz^{-1}/2)+O(z^{-2}).

(25)

We also use the fact from binomial series that

(1-z^{-1})^{p}=1-pz^{-1}+\frac{p(p-1)}{2}z^{-2}+O(z^{-3}).

(26)

Define
t^{*}:=\sup\left\{t\in[1,T]:\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}\leq 1\right\}
. Thus,
\alpha_{t}\equiv 1-\frac{c\log T}{T}
for all
t>t^{*}
. Note that when
T
becomes large,
t^{*}=\Theta\left(\frac{T}{\log T}\right)
.
To further understand the big-
\Theta
term, note that using (
25
),

\displaystyle\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\frac{T\log(1/\delta)}{c\log T}-\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{\log(1/\delta)c\log T}{2T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)\right)\left(1-\frac{\log(1/\delta)c\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\log(1/\delta)c\log T}{2T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle<1\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{as $T\to\infty$}.

This implies that

t^{*}\geq\log(1/\delta)\left(\frac{T}{c\log T}-1\right).

(27)

To start, we suppose
T>t^{*}
is large enough and decompose the sum as

\displaystyle s_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=2}^{t^{*}}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}+\sum_{t=t^{*}+1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{c\log T}{T}\delta(1-\delta)^{p}\sum_{t=2}^{t^{*}}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}\prod_{i=2}^{t}\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{i}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{\alpha}_{t^{*}}^{p}\frac{c\log T}{T}\sum_{t=t^{*}+1}^{T}\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(t-t^{*})}.

(28)

Now we first focus on the second term in (
28
).

\displaystyle\frac{c\log T}{T}\sum_{t=t^{*}+1}^{T}\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(t-t^{*})}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p}\frac{c\log T}{T}\cdot\frac{1-\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(T-t^{*})}}{1-\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p}\frac{c\log T}{T}\cdot\frac{1-\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(T-t^{*})}}{1-\left(1-\frac{pc\log T}{T}+p(p-1)\frac{c^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{2T^{2}}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{3}}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{p}\left(1-\frac{pc\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)\right)\left(1+\frac{(p-1)c\log T}{2T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)-O\left(\frac{1}{T^{pc/2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\left(1-\frac{(p+1)c\log T}{2T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)

where
(i)
follows from (
26
), and
(ii)
is because
t^{*}=\Theta(T/\log T)
and thus
T-t^{*}>T/2
for large
T
.
Also, for all
t=2,\dots,t^{*}
,

\displaystyle\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta)^{p}\prod_{i=2}^{t}\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{i}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\delta)^{p}\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(t-1)}

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\alpha}_{t^{*}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\delta)^{p}\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(t^{*}-1)}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta)^{p}\left(1+\delta p\frac{c\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)\right)\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{pt^{*}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}(1-\delta)^{p}\left(1+\delta p(1+\log(1/\delta))\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\quad e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1+\delta^{2}p\log(1/\delta)\frac{c\log T}{2T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\delta)^{p}e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1+\delta p\left(1+\log(1/\delta)+(\delta/2)\log(1/\delta)\right)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

Here
(iii)
follows because using (
27
) and (
25
), we have that

\displaystyle\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{pt^{*}}\leq\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p\log(1/\delta)\left(\frac{T}{c\log T}-1\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\delta p\log(1/\delta)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1+\delta^{2}p\log(1/\delta)\frac{c\log T}{2T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

(29)

Thus, the second term in (
28
) satisfies that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t^{*}+1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}(1-\delta)^{p}e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1-\left(\frac{p+1}{2}-\delta p\left(1+\log(1/\delta)+(\delta/2)\log(1/\delta)\right)\right)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

(30)

Now we turn to the first term in (
28
), in which the summation can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{t^{*}}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}\prod_{i=2}^{t}\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{i}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{t^{*}}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t}\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p(t-1)}=:\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{t^{*}-1}q^{t}

where

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle:=\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\left(1-\delta p\frac{c\log T}{T}+\delta^{2}p(p-1)\frac{c^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{2T^{2}}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=1+(1-\delta p)\frac{c\log T}{T}+\left(\frac{\delta^{2}p(p-1)}{2}-\delta p\right)\frac{c^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{T^{2}}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{3}}\right).

Note that by assumption
\delta p<1
.
Also, by definition of
t^{*}
and (
29
),

\displaystyle\delta q^{t^{*}}\leq\left(1-\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{pt^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1+\delta p\log(1/\delta)(1+\delta/2)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{*}-1}q^{t}=\frac{c\log T}{T}\times\frac{\delta q^{t^{*}}-\delta q}{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\log T}{T}\times\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1+\delta p\log(1/\delta)(1+\delta/2)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)-\delta q}{q-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{=}}\frac{c\log T}{T}\times\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}\left(1+\delta p\log(1/\delta)(1+\delta/2)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)-1-(1-\delta p)\frac{c\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)}{(1-\delta p)\frac{c\log T}{T}+\left(\frac{\delta^{2}p(p-1)}{2}-\delta p\right)\frac{c^{2}(\log T)^{2}}{T^{2}}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{3}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}+\left(\frac{\delta p\log(1/\delta)(1+\delta/2)}{1-\delta p}-1\right)\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\left(1+\frac{\delta p-\frac{\delta^{2}p(p-1)}{2}}{1-\delta p}\cdot\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}+\left(\frac{\delta p\log(1/\delta)(1+\delta/2)}{1-\delta p}-1+\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}\cdot\frac{\delta p(1-\delta(p-1)/2)}{1-\delta p}\right)\frac{c\log T}{T}

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

where
(iv)
follows from (
26
).
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{t^{*}}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{p}=(1-\delta)^{p}\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\left(\delta\frac{c\log T}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{*}-1}q^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\delta)^{p}\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\delta)^{p}\bigg(\frac{\delta p\log(1/\delta)(1+\delta/2)}{1-\delta p}-1

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}\left(1+\frac{\delta p(1-\delta(p-1)/2)}{1-\delta p}\right)\bigg)\frac{c\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

(31)

Combining (
30
) and (
31
), we have that

s_{T}\leq\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{p}(1-\delta)^{p}e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}+(1-\delta)^{p}\frac{e^{-p\delta\log(1/\delta)}-1}{1-\delta p}\right)}_{=:s_{\infty}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)\right).

Also, for all large
T
’s, since
s_{\infty}\to\frac{1}{p}
and
\delta\log(1/\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
, when
\delta\ll 1
,

s_{T}\leq\frac{1}{p}-\left(1+\frac{p+1}{2p}\right)\frac{c\log T}{T}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

The proof is now complete.
∎

Appendix I
Proofs in
Section

4

I.1
Proof of
Theorem

3

Fix
t\geq 1
. Using the forward model in (
5
), we have that
q_{t|0,y}
is the p.d.f. of
\mathcal{N}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)x_{0}+\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y,\Sigma_{t|0,y})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)=\frac{1}{q_{t|y}(x)}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla q_{t|0,y}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0|y}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{q_{t|y}(x)}\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0,y}(x|x_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)x_{0}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y)\mathrm{d}Q_{0|y}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=-\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}{q_{t|y}(x)}\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0,y}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|y}(x_{0}).

Thus, the equality for
\nabla\log q_{t|y}
is established because by
Lemma

9
,

(\sigma_{y}^{2}H^{\dagger}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d})^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)=(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H).

To see the optimality with
f_{t,y}^{*}
, fix
t\geq 1
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. First note that
(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)f_{t,y}^{*}(x)=(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx)=0
by
Lemma

9
. Now, suppose that
f_{t,y}=f_{t,y}^{*}+v
such that
(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)f_{t,y}=0\implies(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)v=0
. From the definition of
\Delta_{t,y}
in (
7
),

\Delta_{t,y}(x)=(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)(\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)-\nabla\log q_{t}(x))+(H^{\dagger}H)\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)-f_{t,y}(x)

where

\displaystyle(H^{\dagger}H)\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)-f_{t,y}(x)

\displaystyle=(H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-x)-\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx)-v

\displaystyle=-(H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)x-(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx)-v

\displaystyle=-v

where the last line follows from
Lemma

9
.

Thus, if
v=0
, then
f_{t,y}=f_{t,y}^{*}
, and we have

\Delta_{t,y}=(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)(\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)-\nabla\log q_{t}(x)).

(32)

Also, if
v\neq 0
, since
v
is orthogonal to the space induced by
(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)
, we have

\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(x)\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)(\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)-\nabla\log q_{t}(x))\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert v\right\rVert^{2}

(33)

which is minimized at
v=0
. The proof is now complete.

I.2
Proof of
Theorem

4

Fix
t\geq 2
. Recall that the unconditional score
\nabla\log q_{t}(x)
is

\displaystyle\nabla\log q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{t}(x)}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})q_{t}(x)}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}x+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})q_{t}(x)}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

since
q_{t|0}
is the p.d.f. of
\mathcal{N}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0},(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d})
.

In the first half, we consider the case where
\sigma_{y}^{2}
is known, and thus
f_{t,y}=f_{t,y}^{*}
in (
10
). Note that from
Theorem

3
,

\displaystyle\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}{q_{t|y}(x)}\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0,y}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|y}(x_{0}).

Here we also recall from
Theorem

3
that

\Sigma_{t|0,y}:=\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2}H^{\dagger}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d}.

Since
H^{\dagger}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}
is positive semi-definite, all its eigenvalues are non-negative. Write the eigen-decomposition as
H^{\dagger}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}=P\mathrm{diag}(D_{1},\dots,D_{d})P^{\intercal}
where
D_{1}\geq\dots\geq D_{d}\geq 0,\penalty\ \forall i\in[d]
. Then,
\lambda_{min}(\Sigma_{t|0,y})\geq\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2}D_{d}+1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}\geq 1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}
, and we get

\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\right\rVert\leq\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}.

(34)

Also, from (
6
), with the
f_{t,y}^{*}
in (
10
),

\displaystyle g_{t,y}(x)

\displaystyle=f_{t,y}^{*}(x)+(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\nabla\log q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx\right)-\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)x

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})q_{t}(x)}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx\right)-\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)x

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}{q_{t}(x)}\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-x\right)+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}{q_{t}(x)}\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

where
(i)
follows from
Lemma

9
.
Then, the norm-squared of the score mismatch at time
t\geq 2
is

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t|y}-g_{t,y}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)(\nabla\log q_{t|y}-\nabla\log q_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0,y}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|y}(x_{0})}{q_{t|y}(x)}-\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})x_{0}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})}{q_{t}(x)}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\right\rVert^{2}\int_{x_{a},x_{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lVert x_{a}-x_{b}\right\rVert^{2}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t,y}(x_{a})\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{b})

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}\right\rVert^{2}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{a}\in\mathrm{supp}(Q_{0|y})\\
x_{b}\in\mathrm{supp}(Q_{0})\end{subarray}}\left\lVert x_{a}-x_{b}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}d.

(35)

Here
(ii)
follows from Jensen’s inequality, and
(iii)
follows by (
34
) and from the assumption that
Q_{0}
has bounded support (and thus also for both
Q_{0|t}
and
Q_{0|t,y}
). Therefore, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
) (cf.
Lemma

6
), since
1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}\geq 1-\delta
which is a constant,
Assumption

4
is satisfied for all
\sigma_{y}^{2}\geq 0
.
Thus,
Theorem

2
holds with
\gamma=1
and
r=2
.

Now, we consider the case where
\sigma_{y}^{2}
is unknown, and the conditional sampler of interest is
g_{t,y}^{N}(x)=f_{t,y}^{N}(x)+(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\nabla\log q_{t}(x)
where
f_{t,y}^{N}(x)=(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx\right)
. With the same notation as in the proof of
Theorem

3
, we can write
v=f_{t,y}^{N}-f_{t,y}^{*}=((1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}I_{d}-\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1})\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx\right)
. Note that
v
still satisfies that
(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)v=0
.
Using the result in (
33
), we have

\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}^{N}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)(\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x)-\nabla\log q_{t}(x))\right\rVert^{2}\\
+\left\lVert(\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}-(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}I_{d})\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}Hx\right)\right\rVert^{2}

where the first term is the same as in (
35
) which can be upper-bounded in a similar way. To upper-bound the second term, note that by Woodbury matrix identity,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}I_{d}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\left\lVert H^{\dagger}\left(I_{p}+\frac{\sigma_{y}^{2}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}H^{\dagger}\right)^{-1}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}

(36)

where the inequality follows because
\left\lVert H^{\dagger}\right\rVert\lesssim 1
is a constant and the minimum eigenvalue of
(I_{p}+\frac{\sigma_{y}^{2}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}(H^{\dagger})^{\intercal}H^{\dagger})
is at least 1.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}-(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}I_{d})\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}HX_{t}\right)\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{y}^{4}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}HX_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{y}^{4}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{0|y}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|0,y}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}HX_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{y}^{4}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{0|y}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}HX_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{y}^{4}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{0|y}}\left[\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}HX_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|0}}\left\lVert H^{\dagger}H(X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{y}^{4}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{0|y}}\left[\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y\right\rVert^{2}+\bar{\alpha}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(v)}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{y}^{4}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}d

where
(iv)
follows from (
36
), and
(v)
follows from the fact that
Q_{0|y}
has bounded support. Similarly, for general moments
\ell\geq 2
,

\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{t|0,y}^{-1}-(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}I_{d})\left(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}HX_{t}\right)\right\rVert^{\ell}\lesssim\left(\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}d\right)^{\ell/2}.

Therefore, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
), since
1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}\geq 1-\delta
which is a constant, we still have that
Assumption

4
is satisfied (see
Lemma

6
), and
Theorem

2
still holds with
\gamma=1
and
r=4
. The proof is now complete.

I.3
Theorem

6
and its proof

Before we enter the proof of
Propositions

1
and
5
, we first state a similar set of results for Gaussian
Q_{0}
, which turns out to be useful for analyzing Gaussian mixture
Q_{0}
’s. To begin, the following lemma investigates
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}
when
Q_{0}
is Gaussian. This quantity is proportional to the asymptotic bias
\mathcal{W}_{\text{bias}}
.

Proposition 2
.

For
Q_{0}=\mathcal{N}(\mu_{0},\Sigma_{0})
, if
f_{t,y}=f_{t,y}^{*}
in (
10
) and
H=\begin{pmatrix}I_{p}&0\end{pmatrix}
, with the
\alpha_{t}
’s according to
Definition

1
,
Assumption

4
is satisfied, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\frac{\max\{\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d(\lambda_{1}+\sigma_{y}^{2}),d\}}{\min\{\lambda_{d},1\}^{2}\min\{\tilde{\lambda}_{d-p},1\}^{2}}\left\lVert[\Sigma_{0}]_{y\bar{y}}[\Sigma_{0}]_{\bar{y}y}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\cdot(\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d)

where
\lambda_{1}
is the largest eigenvalue of
\Sigma_{0}
, and
\lambda_{d}
and
\tilde{\lambda}_{d-p}
are the smallest eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{0}
and
[\Sigma_{0}]_{\bar{y}\bar{y}}
, respectively.

Proof.

See
Section

J.1
.
∎

With this lemma, the following theorem characterizes the conditional KL divergence when
Q_{0}
is Gaussian.

Theorem 6
.

Suppose that
\sigma_{y}^{2}>0
. Suppose that
1
and
5
hold.
Under the same conditions as in
Proposition

2
,
if
\alpha_{t}
further satisfies
\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\bar{\alpha}_{t}=1+o(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(Q_{0|y}\|\widehat{P}_{0|y})\lesssim(\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d)\frac{(\log T)^{2}}{T}+\sqrt{\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d}\cdot(\log T)\varepsilon.

Note that a similar result can be obtained for
\mathrm{KL}(Q_{1|y}\|\widehat{P}_{1|y})
(where
\mathrm{W}_{2}(Q_{1|y},Q_{0|y})^{2}\lesssim\delta d
) for any general
\sigma_{y}^{2}\geq 0
using the
\alpha_{t}
in (
8
) (see
Remark

1
).

I.3.1
Proof of
Theorem

6

Throughout the proof we use the same notations as in (
55
). Since
Assumption

4
is satisfied from
Proposition

2
, in order to invoke
Theorem

1
, we still need to check
Assumption

3
. Since each
Q_{t|y}\penalty\ (\forall t\geq 0)
is Gaussian, all partial derivatives of its log-p.d.f. higher than third-order equal zero. For the first and second-order, note that
\Sigma_{t|y}=\bar{\alpha}_{t}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{0}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d}+\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2}H^{\dagger}H
. Thus, when
\sigma_{y}^{2}>0
,
\lambda_{min}(\Sigma_{t-1|y})\geq\min\{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}+\bar{\alpha}_{t}\sigma_{y}^{2},1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}+\bar{\alpha}_{t}\tilde{\lambda}_{d}\}\geq\min\{1,\sigma_{y}^{2},\tilde{\lambda}_{d}\}>0
, which yields

\left\lVert\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}\right\rVert\lesssim 1,\quad\forall t\geq 1.

(37)

Thus, we have,
\forall\ell\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t|y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}(X_{t}-\mu_{t|y})\right\rVert^{\ell}\leq\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert^{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(X_{t}-\mu_{t|y})\right\rVert^{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim d^{\ell/2}=O(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))\right\rVert^{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}(m_{t,y}(X_{t})-\mu_{t|y})\right\rVert^{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert^{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(X_{t}-\mu_{t|y})\right\rVert^{\ell}+\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}\right\rVert^{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t|y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim d^{\ell/2}=O(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log q_{t|y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{\ell}=\left\lVert\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}\right\rVert^{\ell}=O(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))\right\rVert^{\ell}=\left\lVert\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}\right\rVert^{\ell}=O(1).

Thus,
Assumption

3
holds when
1-\alpha_{t}
satisfies
Definition

1
.

Now, we can invoke
Theorem

1
and get
\mathrm{KL}(Q_{0|y}\|\widehat{P}_{0|y})\lesssim\mathcal{W}_{\text{oracle}}+\mathcal{W}_{\text{bias}}+\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
,
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\text{oracle}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))\nabla^{2}\log q_{t|y}(X_{t})\Big)\right]+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\text{bias}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\bigg[(\nabla\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log q_{t|y}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\bigg]

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left[\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{3!{\alpha_{t}}^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\bigg[3\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iii}\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})^{i}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial^{3}_{iij}\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})^{j}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\max_{t\geq 1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}(\log T)\varepsilon.

We first consider the estimation error (in both
\mathcal{W}_{\text{oracle}}
and
\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
), which can be upper-bounded as

\max_{t\geq 1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}(\log T)\varepsilon+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim\left(\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log T)\varepsilon

from
Proposition

2
.
Also, when
Q_{t|y}
is Gaussian, we can calculate, for any
x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(x_{t}))\nabla^{2}\log q_{t|y}(x_{t})\Big)=\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}(\alpha_{t}\Sigma_{t-1|y}+(1-\alpha_{t})I_{d})^{-1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}(\alpha_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-2}+O((1-\alpha_{t})^{2})))

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-2})

where
(i)
follows from Taylor expansion when
1-\alpha_{t}
is small. Using (
37
), this implies that

\mathcal{W}_{\text{oracle}}\lesssim\frac{d(\log T)^{2}}{T}+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2}.

Also, from the condition on
\alpha_{t}
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left\lVert\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim(\left\lVert H^{\dagger}y-H^{\dagger}H\mu_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+d).

Now we focus on
\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
(except the estimation error).
Since
Q_{t|y}
is Gaussian, all third-order partial derivatives are zero, and only the first two terms in
\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
remain.
In the following we fix
t\geq 1
. Also recall from (
56
) that when
H=\begin{pmatrix}I_{p}&0\end{pmatrix}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{t,y}

\displaystyle=-\bar{\alpha}_{t}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t,sig}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{0}(H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0})

\displaystyle=-\bar{\alpha}_{t}(\bar{\alpha}_{t}[\Sigma_{0}]_{\bar{y}\bar{y}}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d-p})^{-1}[\Sigma_{0}]_{\bar{y}y}[\Sigma_{t}^{-1}]_{y:}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0}).

For the first term of
\mathcal{W}_{\text{vanish}}
, we first calculate for each
x_{t}
that

\displaystyle\nabla\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|y})-\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}(m_{t,y}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}\mu_{0|y})

Recall that

\displaystyle m_{t,y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{t}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x_{t})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{t}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|y}),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{t}\Sigma_{t-1|y}+(1-\alpha_{t})I_{d})^{-1}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-2}+O((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\nabla\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y})-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-2}\right)(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|y})

\displaystyle\qquad-\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{t}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\nabla\log q_{t|y}(x_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}\mu_{0|y}\right)+O((1-\alpha_{t})^{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3/2}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-2}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|y})+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|y})+O((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}).

Combining with the definition for
\Delta_{t,y}
in (
56
) and using
Lemma

9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\left[\Delta_{t,y}(X_{t})^{\intercal}(\nabla\log q_{t-1|y}(m_{t,y}(X_{t}))-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\nabla\log q_{t|y}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle=\bar{\alpha}_{t}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t|y}}\bigg[(X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0})^{\intercal}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{0}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)\Sigma_{t,sig}^{-1}(I_{d}-H^{\dagger}H)

\displaystyle\qquad\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3/2}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-2}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Sigma_{t-1|y}^{-1}\Sigma_{t|y}^{-1}\right)(X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|y})\bigg]+O((1-\alpha_{t})^{2})
